Read completed /work/sim_reference/mult_add_int_18x19.v
input
{
 "file_path": "/work/sim_reference/mult_add_int_18x19.v"
}
output
1	// mult_add_int_18x19: result = ax*ay + bx + chainin, unsigned, the product and bx zero-extended
2	// to 64 bits, modulo 2^64 (the sum wraps at the result width).
3	// Ports: clk, reset, mode_sigs[12], ax[18], ay[19], bx[36], chainin[64], scanin[19] in;
4	// result[64], chainout[64], scanout[19] out. mode_sigs and scanin are not read (the scan chain is
5	// unused and tied to 0 in every spec).
6	// Registers (the arch's annotation read literally, decision 2026-10-16): every input is registered
7	// at the block's entry at one clock edge (ax, ay, bx and chainin alike); the product and
8	// the sum are formed from those registers and loaded into result at the next edge.
9	// Cycles: ax, ay, bx and chainin to result 2. chainout is combinational from the input
10	// registers: the sum result loads at the next edge unless that edge clears it (1 cycle from the
11	// inputs).
12	// scanout is the ay register (ay to scanout 1), as before; nothing specifies it.
13	// Reset: synchronous, active high (2026-10-17: the arch's annotation does not distinguish a reset
14	// register from a clear sampled by the registers themselves): at a clock edge that samples it high
15	// every register of the block (operand registers, chainin register, result) is cleared to 0; the block
16	// loads again at the next edge that samples it low. No register has an initial value: every one is
17	// X until the first clear.
18	module mult_add_int_18x19 (
19	    input clk,
20	    input reset,
21	    input [11:0] mode_sigs,
22	    input [17:0] ax,
23	    input [18:0] ay,
24	    input [35:0] bx,
25	    input [63:0] chainin,
26	    input [18:0] scanin,
27	    output reg [63:0] result,
28	    output [63:0] chainout,
29	    output [18:0] scanout
30	);
31	
32	    // stage 1: the input registers
33	    reg [17:0] ax_reg;
34	    reg [18:0] ay_reg;
35	    reg [35:0] bx_reg;
36	    reg [63:0] chainin_reg;
37	
38	    // combinational middle: the product, the addend and chainin, from the registers
39	    wire [36:0] prod = ax_reg * ay_reg;                          // 18x19 -> 37, exact
40	    wire [63:0] sum  = prod + bx_reg + chainin_reg;              // zero-extended, modulo 2^64
41	
42	    always @(posedge clk) begin
43	        if (reset) begin
44	            result <= 0;
45	            ax_reg <= 0; ay_reg <= 0; bx_reg <= 0; chainin_reg <= 0;
46	        end
47	        else begin
48	            ax_reg <= ax; ay_reg <= ay; bx_reg <= bx; chainin_reg <= chainin;
49	            result <= sum;                                       // stage 2
50	        end
51	    end
52	
53	    assign chainout = sum;                                  // the sum of the input registers
54	    assign scanout  = ay_reg;
55	
56	endmodule
57

prompt

2 paragraph breaks restored by the export, where the recorder joined text blocks with no separator.

Task

You are the planner. Read the design source, stereovision1.v, and decide which of the candidate arithmetic regions listed below to rewrite onto the hard block described next, or that none should be rewritten. Write a plan for the editor, who carries it out; do not edit any file.

The block
  • primitive to instantiate: mult_add_int_18x19 (precision INT19)
  • computes: out = ax*ay + bx + chainin
  • input ports: clk[1] (clock), reset[1], mode_sigs[12], ax[18], ay[19], bx[36], chainin[64], scanin[19]
  • output ports: result[64], chainout[64], scanout[19]
  • arithmetic: the block reads each operand port as an unsigned number, and its result is unsigned. An operand narrower than its port is zero-extended to the port width. The result wraps at 64 bits: a value past the result width keeps its low 64 bits (modulo 2^64), with no saturation and no flag.
  • registers: on clk, the block registers its operands (ax, ay, bx) at one clock edge and result at the next, with the arithmetic between the two registers: operands applied before clock edge k appear in result after edge k+1, 2 cycles later. The block has no clock enable and no path around either register. chainin is registered at the block's entry like an operand and reaches result 2 cycles after it is applied. chainout is combinational from the input registers: it carries the value result loads at the next clock edge unless that edge clears it, one cycle before result shows it.
  • reset: reset is synchronous and active high: at a clock edge that samples it high it clears every register of the block (the operand registers, the chainin register and the result register) to 0, and the block loads again at the next edge that samples it low.
  • power-up: the block's registers have no initial value. Each loads its input at every clock edge where reset is low, so, for a block whose inputs, chainin included, are defined, the result is defined from the second edge after power-up, with or without a reset. A rewrite may not rely on the result before then.
  • tie-off: chainin = 0 when the block is not chained (a dedicated chain input: only another block's chainout or a constant may drive it); scanin = 0 always (the operand scan chain is not used: ay is driven directly, and scanout is left unconnected); mode_sigs = 0 always (the architecture gives the port no meaning for this mode).
  • connections: connect every input port, by the port names above. chainout reaches only the next block's chainin, through a dedicated connection to the block directly below it in the column; it has no connection to general routing, so nothing else may read it.
The block on this architecture

These are read from the architecture file.

element on a pathns
into the block: a path ends at the block's input register (setup)0.019
inside the block: input register clock-to-Q + delay + output register setup0.060 + 2.140 + 0.019 = 2.219
out of the block: a path starts at the output register of result (clock-to-Q)0.060
chainout: input register clock-to-Q + delay, to the output pin0.060 + 2.140 = 2.200
soft flip-flop, clock to output0.060
  • Blocks in series are separate stages. A path through chainout starts at an input register and continues, in the same stage, into what it drives.
  • A block of this mode takes a DSP tile alone.
  • The block has chain ports: a block's chainout can drive the next block's chainin directly, so a sum can continue from block to block.
  • The device has 306 DSP tiles.
  • One DSP tile's area is 253779 minimum-width transistor areas and one CLB's 27905.
The design
Candidate regions

An id names a product by its module and its multiply. One that ends in (sum_in=<module>) is a product whose sum sits in that other module: the product leaves its own module through a port and is added there, so the arithmetic to convert spans both. A #1, #2 after an id separates instances of the same product that sit in sums of different shapes.

In every row, operation: sop.
In every row, register stages between the products and their sum: products registered 1 stage(s) before the sum.
In every row, products in the whole sum this one belongs to: 4 products in one tree of adders.

idinstances
corr.$mul$stereovision1.v:1374$78(sum_in=sv_chip1_hierarchy_no_mem)64
corr.$mul$stereovision1.v:1375$79(sum_in=sv_chip1_hierarchy_no_mem)64
corr_seq.$mul$stereovision1.v:1867$104(sum_in=sv_chip1_hierarchy_no_mem)12
corr_seq.$mul$stereovision1.v:1868$105(sum_in=sv_chip1_hierarchy_no_mem)12
Correctness: these must hold
  • Same behavior. The rewritten design must produce the same outputs as the original on every cycle, with every register of the original starting at 0. Do not change what the design computes. Logic added only to reproduce it (recomputing a value, deriving the block's reset, correcting a block's result) is allowed.
  • Visibility. Only what the block's output ports carry is visible outside it. A value read by anything else (another expression, a module port, another module) cannot go inside a block: keep it outside, or recompute it. Check the source for every reader.
  • Terms. Keep every term of the original arithmetic. A term with no matching block port is computed outside the block. A port the design ties to a constant may be tied the same way on the block.
  • Registers and reset. The block's registers, if it has any, behave as the contract states. A design register may become a block register only if that keeps its behavior. Drive the block's reset so that "same behavior" holds after every reset, not only at start-up. Registers the rewrite adds start unknown; do not rely on their initial value.
  • Assumptions. If the rewrite is correct only under an assumption about the design's inputs or use (a bound on a value, when a signal is asserted), state it on a line starting ASSUMES: and cite the lines of the design that support it. The reference testbench does not know your assumption, so the rewrite must still match the original on the testbench's stimulus, which does not respect it.
  • Scope. Change only what the rewrite onto the block needs.
Objective

Minimize area × delay against the original design. Among rewrites that lower it, choose the one that lowers it most.

Output
Tiers
  • T1: The block replaces the region's arithmetic as it stands: the same operations on the same operands, now inside the block.
  • T2: The region's arithmetic is regrouped to fit the block (products regrouped into the block's sums, registers moved), computing the same values.
  • NONE: You propose no rewrite this time. The plan states why, from what you were given: no correct rewrite, or none expected to lower area × delay, or the best result so far is the best available. The run then ends and keeps the best result so far if its median over every seed is below the original's, and the original design otherwise (also when no rewrite has beaten it).
Header

Write exactly this header, each key at the start of its own line, then the plan:

TIER: <exactly one of T1, T2, NONE>
REGIONS: <the converted candidate ids, comma-separated; NONE when TIER is NONE>
SHARED_REDUCTION: <the signal or module the converted products are summed in; NONE for a single region>
BLOCKS: <how many blocks the rewrite instantiates, one integer>
MAPPING: <which design signals go to which block ports>
PLAN: <the plan, stating everything listed below>

What the plan must state
  • Every candidate region, converted or left, with the reason each is left; candidates that share a reason may be grouped.
  • How many blocks the rewrite adds.
  • For each converted region, the register stages from its operands to its result, before and after the rewrite.
Output rules
  • Write nothing before the TIER line; read the source first.
  • Your reply is read by the framework, not answered by a person, so do not end with a question or an offer.
  • If a change to the design itself, not to the rewrite, would let more of it use the block, you may say so on a line starting DESIGN_CHANGE:. It is reported to the user and does not change your decision.

PLAN:

What is being converted. All four candidate regions are converted; none is left. The four products of one tree are in_l_re*in_r_re and in_l_im*in_r_im of the _p correlator and of the _n correlator; the two pair sums meet in the top module's 18-bit adder (corr_out_1K <= sext18(corr_out_1_pK) + sext18(corr_out_1_nK), lines 316-336 etc.). Only two module bodies have to be edited — corr (lines 1352-1387) and corr_seq (lines 1829-1871) — and every instance of them picks the change up: 64 corr + 12 corr_seq instances × 2 blocks = 152 blocks, within the 306 DSP tiles.

Why the block's adder is not used (no chaining). chainout is combinational from the input registers, so a chained pair needs the second block's operands applied one cycle after the first block's; the pair sum then lands at result 3 cycles after the operands instead of 2. The design has exactly two unconditional register stages between the operands and the pair sum (lrexrre_reg/limxrim_reg, then corr_out_tmp); everything downstream of corr_out_tmp is three cascaded registers all gated by the same wen/new_data enable (corr.corr_out 1379, wrapper_corr_*.corr_out_K 1283/1626, top corr_out_1K 316), and an enable-gated stage cannot absorb an unconditional extra cycle. So the extra cycle cannot be paid for anywhere and chaining is not correct here. Each block therefore carries one product with bx and chainin tied off, and the 16-bit pair adder stays in soft logic — unchanged arithmetic, unchanged latency.

Edit for corr (lines 1362-1386).

  • Delete the registers lrexrre_reg, limxrim_reg, corr_out_tmp (lines 1367-1369) and their assignments (1374, 1375, 1385). They are read nowhere else in the file (checked: only inside corr). The unused wire declarations in_l_re_reg..in_r_im_reg (1363-1366) are undriven dead code; leave them or delete them, either is fine.
  • Add wire [63:0] re_prod, im_prod; and the two block instances listed in MAPPING.
  • Add wire [2*sh_reg_w-1:0] corr_sum = re_prod[2*sh_reg_w-1:0] + im_prod[2*sh_reg_w-1:0]; (i.e. re_prod[15:0] + im_prod[15:0], 16-bit wrap, same as the original + on two 16-bit regs).
  • The always block keeps only if (new_data) corr_out <= corr_sum; else corr_out <= corr_out;.

Edit for corr_seq (lines 1839-1870).

  • Keep in_l_re_reg, in_l_im_reg, in_r_re_reg, in_r_im_reg and their new_data-enabled loading exactly as they are (lines 1850-1865): the block has no clock enable, so these enabled registers must stay outside and feed ax/ay.
  • Delete lrexrre_reg, limxrim_reg, corr_out_tmp and lines 1867-1869; add the two blocks and corr_sum as above; keep corr_out <= corr_sum under new_data.

Port tie-offs. bx = 36'b0, chainin = 64'b0 (not chained), scanin = 19'b0, mode_sigs = 12'b0, chainout/scanout unconnected. reset = 1'b0: the registers being replaced (lrexrre_reg, limxrim_reg, corr_out_tmp) have no reset in the original — corr/corr_seq have no rst port and the top's rst never reaches them — so driving the block's reset from rst would clear state the original keeps and would break equivalence after every mid-run reset pulse the testbench applies (reset_pattern, random resets). Tying it low reproduces the original exactly. Instantiate mult_add_int_18x19 by name only; its model is supplied by the flow (sim_reference/mult_add_int_18x19.v) — do not copy the definition into stereovision1.v.

Register stages, before and after (both converted modules).

  • corr, before: operands in_l_re/in_r_re/in_l_im/in_r_im → 1 stage (lrexrre_reg, limxrim_reg) → 2nd stage (corr_out_tmp) → enabled corr_out. Two unconditional stages to the pair sum.
  • corr, after: operands → block input register (stage 1) → block result register (stage 2) → combinational 16-bit add → enabled corr_out. Still two unconditional stages; total latency unchanged.
  • corr_seq, before: enabled in_*_reg → lrexrre_reg/limxrim_reg → corr_out_tmp → enabled corr_out.
  • corr_seq, after: enabled in_*_reg (kept) → block input register → block result register → combinational add → enabled corr_out. Unchanged.
  • Downstream stages (wrapper_corr_* output register, the top's 18-bit sign-extended adder register) are untouched.

Correctness argument. Operands are 8-bit unsigned (input[7:0]), zero-extended into ax[18]/ay[19]; the block's unsigned 18×19 product is exact for 8×8, so result[15:0] equals the original 16-bit lrexrre_reg/limxrim_reg exactly. Timing: the block loads its operands at edge k and result at edge k+1, so re_prod after edge k+1 equals what lrexrre_reg held after edge k; hence corr_sum after edge k+1 equals corr_out_tmp after edge k+1, bit for bit, including the 16-bit wrap. The enabled corr_out samples corr_sum on exactly the same edges as before, so nothing downstream (the 18-bit sign-extended tree in the top, port_bus_1to0, the SRAM path) sees any change. Power-up: the blocks' registers are X for the first two edges, but corr_out is the only soft register reading them and its enable new_data (= vidin_new_data_scld_1/2/4_2to3_left from port_bus_2to1_1, lines 955-1014) is a register that is 0 at power-up and can first be 1 only at the third edge, because it is set from counter_out_tmp which is itself 0 at power-up and cleared by rst. By that edge both result registers are already loaded and defined. So no X can reach an output, with or without a reset, and no assumption about the stimulus is needed. No ASSUMES: line is required.

Expected effect on area × delay. 152 soft 8×8 multipliers plus their 16-bit product registers and the 76 corr_out_tmp registers leave the soft fabric (≈150 multipliers × ~100 LUT/adder BLEs, plus ~3600 flip-flops), replaced by 152 DSP tiles (152 × 253779 ≈ 38.6 Mmwta) and 76 16-bit combinational adders — roughly area-neutral. The win is the clock period: the design's longest register-to-register path is the soft 8×8 multiply (it sits alone between sh_reg/in_*_reg and lrexrre_reg), and it is replaced by the block's fixed 2.219 ns stage (0.060 + 2.140 + 0.019) fed from a flip-flop through 0.019 ns of setup; the new soft paths out of the block (0.060 clk-to-Q + 16-bit add + setup, and the untouched 18-bit top-level adder) are well under that. All four regions must go together: leaving any one of them would leave a soft 8×8 multiply on the critical path and forfeit the whole delay gain.

DESIGN_CHANGE: corr/corr_seq's corr_out, wrapper_corr_*'s corr_out_K and the top's corr_out_1K are three cascaded registers all gated by the same enable. If one of them were unconditional (or one unconditional pipeline stage were added anywhere after corr_out_tmp), the two products of each correlator could be chained through chainout/chainin with one soft delay register on the second block's operands, which would move the pair sum into the block and remove all 76 16-bit soft adders at no extra DSP cost; and the top's 18-bit _p+_n adder could then also be folded if the pair sums were not truncated to 16 bits before sign extension.

Reference simulation

After the edit, the framework simulates the edited design against the original with the reference testbench, comparing every output on every cycle after the testbench's reset phase (its first 4 cycles). A candidate that does not match is not measured. The testbench (tb_equiv_sv1_top.v) and the block's behavioural model (mult_add_int_18x19.v) are in sim_reference/.

Read these files in this directory: stereovision1.v

response

I'll read the source to reconsider.

TIER: NONE
REGIONS: NONE
SHARED_REDUCTION: NONE
BLOCKS: 0
MAPPING: none — no block is instantiated; the design is kept as given.
PLAN:

Decision. No rewrite onto mult_add_int_18x19 is proposed. The first pass converted all four candidate regions, was correct (it simulated and was measured), and lowered the period by 32%, but its area × delay is 1.437 × the original's. From the measured numbers, no variant of this rewrite — full or partial — can get below 1.0, so the run should keep the original design.

Every candidate, and why each is left.

All four candidates — corr.$mul$stereovision1.v:1374$78, corr.$mul$stereovision1.v:1375$79, corr_seq.$mul$stereovision1.v:1867$104, corr_seq.$mul$stereovision1.v:1868$105 (all sum_in=sv_chip1_hierarchy_no_mem) — are left for one shared reason: the block's one-block-per-tile area cost is ~3.6× what these multiplies already cost in the baseline, and the delay they can buy back is bounded well above the break-even point. The grounds, from the measurement:

  • The baseline already puts these multiplies in DSP tiles. Baseline: 42 DSP tiles = 42 × 253779 = 1.066e7, leaving 1.496e7 of CLB area (≈536 CLBs). Rewrite: 152 DSP tiles = 3.857e7, leaving 1.555e7 of CLB (≈557 CLBs). The soft area did not fall — it rose slightly. So the 152 8×8 multiplies were not in soft logic to begin with; they were packed roughly 3.6 to a tile, and the rewrite re-spread the same 152 multiplies over 152 whole tiles. The entire +111% area is that repacking, with no soft-logic saving to offset it.
  • 152 blocks is the floor for this arithmetic. Each block computes one ax*ay; the two products of a correlator share no operand, so the two-multiplies-in-one-multiplier packing does not apply. Packing the dot product as ax = x·2^k + u, ay = y + v·2^k yields P = u·y + (x·y + u·v)·2^k + x·v·2^2k, and clean extraction of the middle field needs k ≥ 16 while the ports allow only k ≤ 10 (ax[18], ay[19], 8-bit operands) — the low term u*y overlaps the wanted sum, and removing it outside the block would need another multiply. bx/chainin add terms, they do not multiply, so neither can absorb a second product. One block per product, 2 per correlator instance, (64 + 12) × 2 = 152, is irreducible. Chaining changes which adder does the sum, not the block count.
  • Break-even is out of reach. At the measured rewrite area of 5.4117e7, matching the original's 1.40192e8 needs a period of 2.590 ns. The block's own stage is fixed at 0.060 + 2.140 + 0.019 = 2.219 ns, so the period floor is ≈2.28 ns even with zero routing; the measured period was 3.72162 ns, of which 3.470 ns is wire on a single soft dff → .names → dff net (li5146/lo5146) — routing stretched by scattering 152 of the 306 DSP tiles across the device. Closing a 1.13 ns gap that is almost entirely one long route, on a layout the conversion itself stretched, is not something a plan revision can direct; seed noise is only ~7%.
  • Partial conversion is strictly worse, not a hedge. Converting a subset (e.g. only the 12 corr_seq instances, 24 blocks) adds DSP tiles — 25.6e6 − 1.7e6 + 6.1e6 ≈ 3.0e7, +17% area — while the unconverted corr instances keep the original critical path, so the period stays at ~5.47 ns and the product rises by about the area increase. The delay win only appears when every one of the four regions goes, which is exactly the case that costs 152 tiles. There is no intermediate point on this curve.

Blocks added: 0.

Register stages: no region is converted, so no stage count changes. For the record, the structure that would have to be matched is unchanged from the source: in corr (stereovision1.v:1371-1386), operands → lrexrre_reg/limxrim_reg → corr_out_tmp → new_data-enabled corr_out, i.e. two unconditional stages to the pair sum; in corr_seq (stereovision1.v:1848-1870), the new_data-enabled in_*_reg stage precedes the same two unconditional stages.

No assumption is relied on, since nothing is rewritten.

DESIGN_CHANGE: The obstacle is area, not correctness: these 152 8×8 multiplies are too small for a mode that occupies a whole DSP tile, and the baseline fracturable packing already beats it 3.6-to-1. The design change that would make the block pay is widening or merging the correlator arithmetic so that one tile's worth of multiplier is actually used — e.g. accumulating the 21-way v_corr_20_* correlation sums as a chained sum-of-products at full width instead of truncating each pair sum to 16 bits in corr/corr_seq and re-adding in sign-extended 18-bit adders in sv_chip1_hierarchy_no_mem (lines 316-336, 368-378, 400-405). Separately, corr/corr_seq's corr_out, wrapper_corr_*'s corr_out_K and the top's corr_out_1K are three cascaded registers all gated by the same enable; making one of them unconditional would allow chainout→chainin chaining of each correlator's two products (the extra cycle chainout costs has nowhere to be absorbed today), folding the 76 soft 16-bit pair adders into the blocks — but that saves soft area only, and would not by itself close the DSP-tile gap above.

Objective loop

1 rewrite(s) measured · stopped by: planner declined

loop stopped by
planner declined (no representative mapping)
objective
area x delay (delay register to register, 2026-11-02)
baseline
layout koios_small · used area 2.56155e+07 · critical path 5.47292 ns · wirelength 153334 · area x delay (delay register to register, 2026-11-02) 1.40192e+08 (read from entry; median per term over the entry's seed runs (its seed_medians; seeds lists them))

The objective is area x delay (delay register to register, 2026-11-02): A times D, from VPR's total used logic block area (I/Os and empty grid tiles excluded), A, the delay D (the register-to-register figure since 2026-10-20) and the total routed wirelength, W (all three recorded under every objective). Each term is the median over the loop's seeds; the objective is formed from the medians. Beside every product stand the ratios against the baseline, A/A0 (area), D/D0 (critical path), W/W0 (wirelength) and their product, so the normalization can be read off. After each measured rewrite, the planner is given every measurement so far and asked for a revision. A revision is built and checked like the first rewrite, and the lowest product is kept, a later rewrite having to beat it by more than 6.95%. The loop stops when the planner declines to revise, when a revision does not lower the best product by more than 6.95%, when a revision fails its checks, or when 9 revisions have been made.

Stated: the BLOCKS, TILES and STAGE_NS lines of the plan header. Measured: the packed DSP tile count from the gate flow, and the median period, area and wirelength over the seeds. "A/A0 · D/D0 · W/W0" are the rewrite's medians over the baseline's, whose product is the "vs baseline" change plus one. "vs previous" is against the rewrite measured before this one. "Path 1 ends in" is read from the rewrite's own timing report and netlist: the constant coefficients on the block (or plain multiply) that path 1 passes through, matched against the constants each module of the edited RTL multiplies by; two modules that share a coefficient set are both named, and the hover text gives the element and its constants. Verification comes from verification.yaml in the run dir when one was written; the loop itself does not run the equivalence checks. Its label is proven when the LEC closed, simulation under assumption when the simulation passed without a closed proof and the plan stated a bound it rests on, simulation only when it stated none; the hover text gives the simulation and LEC results and the stated bounds.

Each row gives the candidate's figures at seed 1, which every measured candidate was measured at, and then, where more seeds were run, the median over the seeds named, which is the figure the loop compared. The period is register to register (I/O pads not timed). The product is the run's objective, area × delay; the ratios are against the baseline's figures (the median of five seeds, 1 to 5; A/A0 area, D/D0 period, and their product). The original design is the result to beat: the run holds it until a rewrite's area × delay is below the original's, and at the end keeps a rewrite only if its median over every seed is still below; it holds the original now, since no row below has beaten it:

I'll read the source to reconsider.

TIER: NONE
REGIONS: NONE
SHARED_REDUCTION: NONE
BLOCKS: 0
MAPPING: none — no block is instantiated; the design is kept as given.
PLAN:

Decision. No rewrite onto mult_add_int_18x19 is proposed. The first pass converted all four candidate regions, was correct (it simulated and was measured), and lowered the period by 32%, but its area × delay is 1.437 × the original's. From the measured numbers, no variant of this rewrite — full or partial — can get below 1.0, so the run should keep the original design.

Every candidate, and why each is left.

All four candidates — corr.$mul$stereovision1.v:1374$78, corr.$mul$stereovision1.v:1375$79, corr_seq.$mul$stereovision1.v:1867$104, corr_seq.$mul$stereovision1.v:1868$105 (all sum_in=sv_chip1_hierarchy_no_mem) — are left for one shared reason: the block's one-block-per-tile area cost is ~3.6× what these multiplies already cost in the baseline, and the delay they can buy back is bounded well above the break-even point. The grounds, from the measurement:

  • The baseline already puts these multiplies in DSP tiles. Baseline: 42 DSP tiles = 42 × 253779 = 1.066e7, leaving 1.496e7 of CLB area (≈536 CLBs). Rewrite: 152 DSP tiles = 3.857e7, leaving 1.555e7 of CLB (≈557 CLBs). The soft area did not fall — it rose slightly. So the 152 8×8 multiplies were not in soft logic to begin with; they were packed roughly 3.6 to a tile, and the rewrite re-spread the same 152 multiplies over 152 whole tiles. The entire +111% area is that repacking, with no soft-logic saving to offset it.
  • 152 blocks is the floor for this arithmetic. Each block computes one ax*ay; the two products of a correlator share no operand, so the two-multiplies-in-one-multiplier packing does not apply. Packing the dot product as ax = x·2^k + u, ay = y + v·2^k yields P = u·y + (x·y + u·v)·2^k + x·v·2^2k, and clean extraction of the middle field needs k ≥ 16 while the ports allow only k ≤ 10 (ax[18], ay[19], 8-bit operands) — the low term u*y overlaps the wanted sum, and removing it outside the block would need another multiply. bx/chainin add terms, they do not multiply, so neither can absorb a second product. One block per product, 2 per correlator instance, (64 + 12) × 2 = 152, is irreducible. Chaining changes which adder does the sum, not the block count.
  • Break-even is out of reach. At the measured rewrite area of 5.4117e7, matching the original's 1.40192e8 needs a period of 2.590 ns. The block's own stage is fixed at 0.060 + 2.140 + 0.019 = 2.219 ns, so the period floor is ≈2.28 ns even with zero routing; the measured period was 3.72162 ns, of which 3.470 ns is wire on a single soft dff → .names → dff net (li5146/lo5146) — routing stretched by scattering 152 of the 306 DSP tiles across the device. Closing a 1.13 ns gap that is almost entirely one long route, on a layout the conversion itself stretched, is not something a plan revision can direct; seed noise is only ~7%.
  • Partial conversion is strictly worse, not a hedge. Converting a subset (e.g. only the 12 corr_seq instances, 24 blocks) adds DSP tiles — 25.6e6 − 1.7e6 + 6.1e6 ≈ 3.0e7, +17% area — while the unconverted corr instances keep the original critical path, so the period stays at ~5.47 ns and the product rises by about the area increase. The delay win only appears when every one of the four regions goes, which is exactly the case that costs 152 tiles. There is no intermediate point on this curve.

Blocks added: 0.

Register stages: no region is converted, so no stage count changes. For the record, the structure that would have to be matched is unchanged from the source: in corr (stereovision1.v:1371-1386), operands → lrexrre_reg/limxrim_reg → corr_out_tmp → new_data-enabled corr_out, i.e. two unconditional stages to the pair sum; in corr_seq (stereovision1.v:1848-1870), the new_data-enabled in_*_reg stage precedes the same two unconditional stages.

No assumption is relied on, since nothing is rewritten.

DESIGN_CHANGE: The obstacle is area, not correctness: these 152 8×8 multiplies are too small for a mode that occupies a whole DSP tile, and the baseline fracturable packing already beats it 3.6-to-1. The design change that would make the block pay is widening or merging the correlator arithmetic so that one tile's worth of multiplier is actually used — e.g. accumulating the 21-way v_corr_20_* correlation sums as a chained sum-of-products at full width instead of truncating each pair sum to 16 bits in corr/corr_seq and re-adding in sign-extended 18-bit adders in sv_chip1_hierarchy_no_mem (lines 316-336, 368-378, 400-405). Separately, corr/corr_seq's corr_out, wrapper_corr_*'s corr_out_K and the top's corr_out_1K are three cascaded registers all gated by the same enable; making one of them unconditional would allow chainout→chainin chaining of each correlator's two products (the extra cycle chainout costs has nowhere to be absorbed today), folding the 76 soft 16-bit pair adders into the blocks — but that saves soft area only, and would not by itself close the DSP-tile gap above.
